# ./catalog API declaration snapshot

Maturity: **experimental**.

Development-preview API; no stable compatibility claim.

Generated by `node --experimental-strip-types ./scripts/api-contract.ts --update`. Every change requires explicit review.

## dist/bff/dispatch.d.ts

```ts
import type { HttpRequest, HttpResponse } from "../http/index.js";
export type RouteEntry = {
    route: string;
    pattern: RegExp;
    params: string[];
    handler: (req: HttpRequest, res: HttpResponse) => unknown;
    /** Fail unsupported hosted bundles closed before auth without weakening direct-route auth. */
    availability?: "direct-postgres";
};
/** Match `routes` against one request and run the handler, or answer not found. */
export type BffDispatch = (routes: readonly RouteEntry[], req: HttpRequest, res: HttpResponse) => Promise<unknown>;
export interface BffDispatcherOptions {
    /** Environment read on each request to decide a route's bundle availability. */
    env: () => Record<string, string | undefined>;
    /** Writes the deployment's not-found response for an unmatched or unavailable path. */
    notFound: (res: HttpResponse, pathname: string) => void;
    /** Runs once per request before matching (for example, ambient request configuration). */
    beforeDispatch?: () => void;
    /**
     * Runs after matching and before the handler, for example route authorization; resolving false
     * means it already answered. Required, so no composition skips it by omission; a deployment
     * without guarded routes passes `() => true`.
     */
    preflight: (req: HttpRequest, res: HttpResponse, route: string) => Promise<boolean> | boolean;
}
/** Build the dispatcher a deployment mounts on its hosted and Node entrypoints alike. */
export declare function createBffDispatcher(options: BffDispatcherOptions): BffDispatch;
```

```ts
import type { HttpResponse } from "../http/index.js";
export declare const BFF_REQUEST_REFERENCE_HEADER = "x-request-id";
export declare const BFF_ERROR_CODES: readonly ["BAD_REQUEST", "UNAUTHORIZED", "FORBIDDEN", "NOT_FOUND", "METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED", "CONFLICT", "RATE_LIMITED", "UPSTREAM_UNAVAILABLE", "INTERNAL", "INVALID_RESPONSE"];
export type BffErrorCode = (typeof BFF_ERROR_CODES)[number];
export type BffMeta = {
    requestId?: string;
    contractVersion?: string;
    [key: string]: unknown;
};
export type BffError = {
    code: BffErrorCode;
    message: string;
    details?: unknown;
};
export type BffSuccessResponse<T> = {
    ok: true;
    data: T;
    meta?: BffMeta;
};
export type BffErrorResponse = {
    ok: false;
    error: BffError;
    meta?: BffMeta;
};
export type BffResponse<T> = BffSuccessResponse<T> | BffErrorResponse;
/** The HTTP status each error code answers with unless the caller names another. */
export declare const BFF_ERROR_STATUS: Readonly<Record<BffErrorCode, number>>;
export interface BffErrorOptions {
    status?: number;
    details?: unknown;
    meta?: BffMeta;
}
export interface BffSuccessOptions {
    cacheControl?: string;
}
export interface BffResponderOptions {
    /** The request reference observed for this response, if the deployment records one. */
    responseRequestId?: (res: HttpResponse) => string | undefined;
}
export interface BffResponder {
    sendBffSuccess<T>(res: HttpResponse, data: T, meta?: BffMeta, options?: BffSuccessOptions): void;
    sendBffError(res: HttpResponse, code: BffErrorCode, message: string, options?: BffErrorOptions): void;
    sendMethodNotAllowed(res: HttpResponse, allowedMethods: readonly string[]): void;
}
/** Build the envelope writers a deployment's handlers share. */
export declare function createBffResponder(options?: BffResponderOptions): BffResponder;
```

```ts
import type { ZodType } from "zod";
import type { HttpRequest, HttpResponse } from "../http/index.js";
/** Structural inputs to the reference admin kit. Authorization and parsing stay with the host. */
export type AuthorizeAdmin = () => Promise<{
    ok: true;
    userId: string;
    role: "admin" | "distributor";
    isMachineActor: boolean;
} | {
    ok: false;
    code: "UNAUTHORIZED" | "FORBIDDEN";
    message: string;
    identityCode?: string;
}>;
export interface AgentDomainQuerySpec<T> {
    readonly key: string;
    readonly requestSchema: ZodType<T, unknown>;
    readonly gate: "read";
    readonly allowedActorKinds: readonly ("human" | "machine")[];
}
export interface AgentDomainMutationSpec<T> extends Omit<AgentDomainQuerySpec<T>, "gate"> {
    readonly gate: "mutation" | "activation";
    readonly lifecycleMarkers: readonly ("LIFECYCLE_ACTIVATE" | "LIFECYCLE_DEACTIVATE" | "HARD_DELETE")[];
}
export type BundleMutationKey = "create" | "update" | "setComposition" | "setTargetPrice" | "archive" | "restore" | "cloneDraft" | "activate" | "deactivate";
export type BundleQueryKey = "list" | "get" | "history" | "previewPrice";
export interface BundleResponseSchema {
    safeParse(value: unknown): {
        success: boolean;
        data?: unknown;
    };
}
type Handler = (req: HttpRequest, res: HttpResponse) => Promise<void>;
interface CommonOptions<T> {
    authorizeAdmin: AuthorizeAdmin;
    flagName?: string;
    enabled?: boolean;
    toResponse(value: T): Record<string, unknown>;
    disabledMessage?: string;
    invalidRequestMessage: string;
    failureMessage: string;
}
export interface BundleAdminBindings {
    bundleSpec: {
        readonly mutations: Readonly<Record<string, AgentDomainMutationSpec<unknown>>>;
        readonly queries?: Readonly<Record<string, AgentDomainQuerySpec<unknown>>>;
        readonly mutationFlag: `${string}_MUTATIONS_ENABLED`;
        readonly activationFlag: `${string}_ACTIVATION_ENABLED`;
        readonly readFlag?: `${string}_READ_ENABLED`;
    };
    COMMERCE_CONTRACT_VERSION: "commerce.v0";
    /** Passed by identity: instanceof and shared SQLSTATE mapping retain their owner. */
    BundleRpcError: new (sqlstate: string | undefined, message: string) => Error & {
        sqlstate: string | undefined;
        pgMessage: string;
    };
    createAdminMutationHandler<Request, Result>(options: CommonOptions<Result> & {
        mutation: AgentDomainMutationSpec<Request>;
        invoke(actorId: string, request: Request): Promise<Result>;
    }): Handler;
    createAdminParameterizedReadHandler<Request, Result>(options: CommonOptions<Result> & {
        query: AgentDomainQuerySpec<Request>;
        load(actorId: string, request: Request): Promise<Result>;
        responseSchema: BundleResponseSchema;
        invalidResponseMessage: string;
    }): Handler;
    bundleHistoryResponseSchema: BundleResponseSchema;
    getBundleResponseSchema: BundleResponseSchema;
    listBundlesResponseSchema: BundleResponseSchema;
    previewBundlePriceResponseSchema: BundleResponseSchema;
}
export {};
```

## dist/catalog/adminBundleWritePort.d.ts

```ts
import type { CreateBundleDraftRequest, SetBundleCompositionRequest, SetBundleTargetPriceRequest, UpdateBundleDraftRequest } from "./contracts/bundleAdmin.js";
import type { ActivateBundleRequest, ArchiveBundleRequest, CloneBundleDraftRequest, DeactivateBundleRequest, RestoreBundleRequest } from "./contracts/bundleAdmin.js";
/**
 * The semantic write port for the admin/agent bundle surface.
 *
 * This file names the CAPABILITY, never the storage. Both shipped write adapters
 * implement exactly this interface and are proved against ONE shared scenario table, so a caller cannot
 * tell them apart and neither can a rule. No data-client type, table name or
 * routine name appears here; the domain layer opens no database handle of its own.
 *
 * Each method is one atomic operation on the security boundary: the boundary takes
 * the actor explicitly; the managed chain re-derives actor kind, while native
 * relies on the handler human-only gate. Each boundary writes its ledger row in the same transaction, short-circuits a replayed
 * idempotency key, and validates a dry run without durable writes.
 */
/** Shape every bundle write returns. */
export interface BundleWriteResult {
    idempotent: boolean;
    dryRun: boolean;
    code?: string;
    bundleId?: string;
    componentCount?: number;
}
/** The opaque rules envelope a bundle carries, as the boundary hands it back. */
export interface BundleCompositionConstraintEnvelope {
    kind: string;
    version: number;
    data: Record<string, unknown>;
}
export interface AdminBundleWritePort {
    /**
     * Read the bundle's stored composition constraint. This is a WRITE-path read: the
     * whole-set replace must be validated against the envelope the bundle already
     * carries before anything is written, and the adopter's `CompositionRulesPort` is
     * the only thing that can decide it. Returns `null` for an unconstrained bundle.
     * Raises P0002 when the bundle does not exist.
     */
    loadCompositionConstraint(code: string): Promise<BundleCompositionConstraintEnvelope | null>;
    /** Create or edit a DRAFT bundle from a full structural payload. */
    upsertDraft(actorId: string, input: CreateBundleDraftRequest): Promise<BundleWriteResult>;
    /**
     * Partial structural edit of a DRAFT bundle. The upsert is full-payload, so this
     * reads the current bundle, overlays the supplied fields and replays the merged
     * payload through the same boundary. Raises NOT_FOUND (P0002) when the bundle
     * does not exist.
     */
    updateDraft(actorId: string, input: UpdateBundleDraftRequest): Promise<BundleWriteResult>;
    /**
     * Replace the bill of materials as a WHOLE SET, atomically. Raises P0002 when the
     * bundle or a named unit is missing, and P0001 when a unit is not sellable on its
     * own or is priced in another currency.
     */
    setComposition(actorId: string, input: SetBundleCompositionRequest): Promise<BundleWriteResult>;
    /**
     * Append a new active target price and close the previous one. Never mutates an
     * existing row. Raises P0001 when the target sits above the sum of the parts or
     * below the floor every component must clear.
     */
    setTargetPrice(actorId: string, input: SetBundleTargetPriceRequest): Promise<BundleWriteResult>;
    /** Remove a bundle from sale (status flip, never a hard delete). P0002 when missing. */
    archive(actorId: string, input: ArchiveBundleRequest): Promise<BundleWriteResult>;
    /**
     * Restore an archived bundle to draft. Raises P0002 when missing and P0001
     * (`restore_requires_archived`) when the bundle is not archived.
     */
    restore(actorId: string, input: RestoreBundleRequest): Promise<BundleWriteResult>;
    /**
     * Clone any bundle into a NEW draft under a new code, including its composition.
     * Raises P0002 (`clone_source_not_found`) when the source is missing and P0001
     * when the target code is already taken.
     */
    cloneDraft(actorId: string, input: CloneBundleDraftRequest): Promise<BundleWriteResult>;
    /**
     * Publish (draft -> active). HUMAN-ONLY: the managed chain raises 42501
     * (`publish_requires_human`) for machine actors; native relies on the handler gate. Raises P0001 when the bundle has
     * no composition or no active target price.
     */
    activate(actorId: string, input: ActivateBundleRequest): Promise<BundleWriteResult>;
    /**
     * Unpublish (active -> draft). HUMAN-ONLY: the managed chain uses the same
     * 42501 refusal; native relies on the handler gate. Raises P0001
     * (`deactivate_requires_active`) when the bundle is not live.
     */
    deactivate(actorId: string, input: DeactivateBundleRequest): Promise<BundleWriteResult>;
}
```
## dist/catalog/bundleAdminWriteStore.d.ts

```ts
import type { AdminBundleWritePort } from "./adminBundleWritePort.js";
/**
 * The marshalling both bundle write adapters share.
 *
 * The two shipped adapters differ in exactly one thing: which client executes a
 * named routine call. Everything else — argument names, result normalization,
 * error translation — is identical, so it lives here once rather than being
 * copied and left to drift. Each adapter supplies its own client and owns its own
 * lifecycle; neither owns a rule.
 */
/** What a client returns, in the one shape both chains already speak. */
type ClientResult = {
    data: unknown;
    error: {
        code?: string;
        message?: string;
    } | null;
};
/**
 * The two capabilities an adapter must supply: execute a named routine, and read
 * one column by equality. The second exists only for the constraint envelope the
 * composition rules must be validated against before anything is written; every
 * decision still belongs to a routine or to the rules port.
 */
export interface BundleWriteRoutineClient {
    rpc(name: string, args: Record<string, unknown>): PromiseLike<ClientResult>;
    from(table: string): {
        select(columns: string): {
            eq(column: string, value: unknown): PromiseLike<ClientResult>;
        };
    };
}
/** The routine names the write boundary publishes, in both chains. */
export declare const BUNDLE_WRITE_ROUTINES: {
    readonly upsertDraft: "admin_upsert_bundle_draft";
    readonly setComposition: "admin_set_bundle_composition";
    readonly setTargetPrice: "admin_set_bundle_target_price";
    readonly archive: "admin_archive_bundle";
    readonly restore: "admin_restore_bundle";
    readonly activate: "admin_activate_bundle";
    readonly deactivate: "admin_deactivate_bundle";
};
/** The relation the constraint envelope is read from, identical in both chains. */
export declare const BUNDLE_RELATION = "catalog_bundles";
/**
 * Build the bundle write port over one routine client. Errors are translated to
 * `BundleRpcError` so the handler's shared mapper turns 42501 into FORBIDDEN,
 * P0001 into a rule conflict and P0002 into NOT_FOUND, identically on both chains.
 */
export declare function createBundleAdminWriteStore(client: BundleWriteRoutineClient): AdminBundleWritePort;
export {};
```
## dist/catalog/bundleAvailability.d.ts

```ts
import type { BundleRuleBindings, BundleStockStatus as CommerceOfferAvailabilityStatus } from "./bundleBindings.js";
/**
 * Derived stock for a composed bundle — the pure kernel that folds many component
 * stock figures into ONE answer about the bundle.
 *
 * A bundle is only as sellable as its scarcest counted part: with a bill of
 * materials of `quantity` units per component, the number of whole bundles that
 * can ship is `min(floor(component.sellableNow / component.quantity))`. That
 * component is named (`limitingSku`) so an operator learns WHICH part is the
 * constraint instead of only that there is one.
 *
 * FAIL TO UNKNOWN, NEVER GUESS. If any counted component's stock is itself
 * unknown, the bundle's stock is unknown — not the minimum of the parts that did
 * answer. Treating a missing figure as unlimited oversells; treating it as zero
 * hides a sellable bundle. Both are decisions this module refuses to make, so it
 * reports `unknown` and names the component whose figure is missing.
 *
 * The status ladder and the reason-code vocabulary are IMPORTED from the commerce
 * domain's PUBLIC port seam rather than restated, so a bundle and a single unit
 * can never disagree about what `low_stock` means.
 *
 * Pure: integer arithmetic, no clock, no randomness, no I/O.
 */
/**
 * Threshold below which a bundle reads as `low_stock`. Deliberately far under the
 * single-unit default: a bundle consumes several units per sale, so the same raw
 * stock represents far fewer bundles and a unit-scale threshold would report
 * nearly every bundle as low.
 */
export declare const DEFAULT_BUNDLE_LOW_STOCK_THRESHOLD = 3;
/** A counted component answered with no figure at all. */
export declare const BUNDLE_COMPONENT_STOCK_UNKNOWN = "component_stock_unknown";
/** Nothing was counted — no non-add-on component to derive an answer from. */
export declare const BUNDLE_COMPOSITION_EMPTY = "bundle_composition_empty";
export interface BundleAvailabilityComponent {
    sku: string;
    /** Units of this component consumed by ONE bundle; always positive. */
    quantity: number;
    isAddon: boolean;
    /** Sellable units of the component, or `null` when the figure is unknown. */
    sellableNow: number | null;
}
export interface BundleAvailability {
    /** Whole bundles that can ship now, or `null` when any counted part is unknown. */
    sellableNow: number | null;
    status: CommerceOfferAvailabilityStatus;
    reasonCode: string;
    /** The component that decided the answer; `null` only when nothing was counted. */
    limitingSku: string | null;
}
export interface DeriveBundleAvailabilityInput {
    components: readonly BundleAvailabilityComponent[];
    lowStockThreshold?: number;
    /**
     * Add-ons are excluded by default: an add-on is priced into the bundle but
     * optional in composition, so its stock must not make the bundle unsellable.
     * An operator who sells the add-on as mandatory opts in here.
     */
    includeAddonsInStock?: boolean;
}
export declare function createBundleAvailability({ offerAvailabilityReasonCode, offerAvailabilityStatusFor }: Pick<BundleRuleBindings, "offerAvailabilityReasonCode" | "offerAvailabilityStatusFor">): {
    deriveBundleAvailability: (input: DeriveBundleAvailabilityInput) => BundleAvailability;
};
```
## dist/catalog/bundleAvailabilityService.d.ts

```ts
import type { BundleAvailability, BundleAvailabilityComponent } from "./bundleAvailability.js";
import type { BundleStockPort as CommerceOfferAvailabilityPort } from "./bundleBindings.js";
import type { ActiveBundleComposition, BundleCatalogReadPort } from "./bundleCatalogReadPort.js";
/**
 * Derived bundle stock, composed from the two ports that already know the halves:
 * the bundle read port knows the bill of materials, the EXISTING commerce offer
 * availability port knows what a unit's stock is. Nothing here re-derives stock and
 * nothing here opens a database handle.
 *
 * Component SKUs are deduplicated across the supplied compositions. When at
 * least one SKU is requested, this service makes one `getAvailability` call and
 * reuses each returned SKU figure across the compositions in that response. An
 * empty composition list or a combined set with no component SKUs makes no call.
 * The injected availability port owns its access and query strategy; this service
 * promises no query count.
 *
 * A sku the availability port does not answer for is a MISSING figure, not zero:
 * it is folded in as `sellableNow: null`, so the pure kernel reports the bundle as
 * unknown and names the component rather than quietly overselling or hiding it.
 */
export interface BundleAvailabilityServiceDeps {
    bundleCatalogReadPort: BundleCatalogReadPort;
    offerAvailabilityPort: CommerceOfferAvailabilityPort;
    /** Threshold handed to the pure kernel; its own default applies when omitted. */
    lowStockThreshold?: number;
    includeAddonsInStock?: boolean;
}
export interface BundleAvailabilityRow {
    composition: ActiveBundleComposition;
    availability: BundleAvailability;
    /**
     * The EXACT rows the kernel was given, carried out again rather than recomputed.
     *
     * The bundle-level answer names one limiting component; an operator staring at a bundle that
     * cannot ship needs to know how far off every OTHER component is too, so they can tell "order one
     * more of this" from "this bundle is months away". These rows are the only place that figure
     * exists — the kernel folds them into a single number and the sellable feed's components carry
     * price, never stock. Returning them costs nothing here and is the alternative to a second stock
     * derivation somewhere that can disagree with this one.
     */
    components: readonly BundleAvailabilityComponent[];
}
export interface BundleAvailabilityService {
    /** Fold stock into a set of compositions the caller already holds. */
    describeCompositions(compositions: readonly ActiveBundleComposition[]): Promise<BundleAvailabilityRow[]>;
    /** Read the live sellable set and fold stock into it. */
    describeActiveBundles(input?: {
        currency?: string;
    }): Promise<BundleAvailabilityRow[]>;
}
export declare function createBundleAvailabilityServices({ deriveBundleAvailability }: {
    deriveBundleAvailability: (input: import("./bundleAvailability.js").DeriveBundleAvailabilityInput) => BundleAvailability;
}): {
    createBundleAvailabilityService: (deps: BundleAvailabilityServiceDeps) => BundleAvailabilityService;
};
/**
 * The port to use where no stock source is bound at all.
 *
 * It answers every item with `sellableNow: null`, which the kernel reads as
 * unknown — so a deployment without an inventory rail publishes bundles whose
 * price is exact and whose stock is honestly unstated. The alternative, omitting
 * the availability block, would have made "we do not know" indistinguishable from
 * "we did not ask", and the alternative to THAT — defaulting to available —
 * oversells on a deployment that never counted anything.
 */
export declare function createUnknownOfferAvailabilityPort(): CommerceOfferAvailabilityPort;
```

## dist/catalog/bundleCatalogQueries.d.ts

```ts
/**
 * The statements the direct-Postgres bundle read adapter renders.
 *
 * They live beside the adapter rather than inside it because they are the part a
 * reader comes here to check — which relations are touched, which columns are
 * asked for, and how a component's reference price is resolved — and reading them
 * should not mean scrolling past the row marshalling that consumes them.
 */
export declare const SUMMARY_COLUMNS = "\n  b.id, b.code, b.title, b.status, b.fulfillment_mode, b.updated_at,\n  b.composition_constraint, b.metadata,\n  (SELECT count(*) FROM public.catalog_bundle_components c WHERE c.bundle_id = b.id)\n    AS component_count,\n  EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.catalog_bundle_prices p WHERE p.bundle_id = b.id AND p.active)\n    AS has_active_target_price";
export declare const LIST_FILTER = "\n  WHERE ($1::text = 'all' OR b.status = $1::text)\n    AND ($2::text IS NULL OR b.code ILIKE '%' || $2 || '%' OR b.title ILIKE '%' || $2 || '%')";
export declare const LIST_SQL = "SELECT \n  b.id, b.code, b.title, b.status, b.fulfillment_mode, b.updated_at,\n  b.composition_constraint, b.metadata,\n  (SELECT count(*) FROM public.catalog_bundle_components c WHERE c.bundle_id = b.id)\n    AS component_count,\n  EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.catalog_bundle_prices p WHERE p.bundle_id = b.id AND p.active)\n    AS has_active_target_price FROM public.catalog_bundles b \n  WHERE ($1::text = 'all' OR b.status = $1::text)\n    AND ($2::text IS NULL OR b.code ILIKE '%' || $2 || '%' OR b.title ILIKE '%' || $2 || '%')\n  ORDER BY b.code LIMIT $3 OFFSET $4";
export declare const COUNT_SQL = "SELECT count(*) AS total FROM public.catalog_bundles b \n  WHERE ($1::text = 'all' OR b.status = $1::text)\n    AND ($2::text IS NULL OR b.code ILIKE '%' || $2 || '%' OR b.title ILIKE '%' || $2 || '%')";
export declare const DETAIL_SQL = "SELECT \n  b.id, b.code, b.title, b.status, b.fulfillment_mode, b.updated_at,\n  b.composition_constraint, b.metadata,\n  (SELECT count(*) FROM public.catalog_bundle_components c WHERE c.bundle_id = b.id)\n    AS component_count,\n  EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.catalog_bundle_prices p WHERE p.bundle_id = b.id AND p.active)\n    AS has_active_target_price FROM public.catalog_bundles b WHERE b.code = $1";
/**
 * The unit's reference price in ONE named list: the lowest tier that is active,
 * newest window first. A unit the list prices nothing for resolves to NULL rather
 * than dropping the component, so a caller sees an unpriced part instead of a
 * shorter bill of materials.
 */
export declare const REFERENCE_PRICE = "(\n  SELECT pe.unit_price_minor FROM public.price_entries pe\n   WHERE pe.variant_id = s.id AND pe.price_list_id = %LIST% AND pe.active\n   ORDER BY pe.min_qty ASC, pe.valid_from DESC LIMIT 1)";
export declare const COMPONENT_SQL: string;
export declare const FEED_COMPONENT_SQL: string;
export declare const PRICE_SQL = "\n  SELECT p.mode, p.target_price_minor, list.currency, p.amount_kind, p.active,\n         p.valid_from, p.valid_to\n    FROM public.catalog_bundle_prices p\n    JOIN public.price_lists AS list ON list.id = p.price_list_id\n   WHERE p.bundle_id = $1::uuid\n   ORDER BY p.valid_from DESC";
export declare const OWN_LIST_SQL = "\n  SELECT list.id, list.currency\n    FROM public.catalog_bundle_prices p\n    JOIN public.price_lists AS list ON list.id = p.price_list_id\n   WHERE p.bundle_id = $1::uuid AND p.active AND ($2::text IS NULL OR p.mode = $2::text)\n   ORDER BY p.valid_from DESC LIMIT 1";
export declare const CURRENCY_LIST_SQL = "\n  SELECT id, currency FROM public.price_lists\n   WHERE currency = $1::text AND status = 'active'\n   ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT 1";
export declare const FEED_SQL = "\n  SELECT b.id, b.code, b.title, b.fulfillment_mode, p.mode, p.target_price_minor,\n         p.price_list_id, list.currency\n    FROM public.catalog_bundles b\n    JOIN public.catalog_bundle_prices p ON p.bundle_id = b.id AND p.active\n    JOIN public.price_lists AS list ON list.id = p.price_list_id\n   WHERE b.status = 'active' AND ($1::text IS NULL OR list.currency = $1::text)\n   ORDER BY b.code, p.valid_from DESC";
export declare const HISTORY_SQL = "\n  SELECT id, bundle_code, action, before_state, after_state, created_at\n    FROM public.catalog_bundle_write_events\n   WHERE bundle_code = $1::text\n   ORDER BY created_at DESC LIMIT $2 OFFSET $3";
export declare const HISTORY_COUNT_SQL = "\n  SELECT count(*) AS total FROM public.catalog_bundle_write_events WHERE bundle_code = $1::text";
```
## dist/catalog/bundleCatalogReadPort.d.ts

```ts
/**
 * The semantic READ port for bundles.
 *
 * This file names the CAPABILITY, never the storage: no table, no routine, no
 * data-client type and no vendor appears here, and the domain layer opens no
 * database handle of its own. Both shipped read adapters implement exactly this
 * interface and are proved against ONE shared scenario table, so a caller cannot
 * tell them apart. It is the same shape the write port
 * (`adminBundleWritePort.ts`) established in the previous wave.
 *
 * Reads are plain SELECTs rather than security-definer routines: the write
 * boundary is where authority lives, and a read that re-implemented it would be a
 * second place for the same rules to be written down. The admin surface reads the
 * whole lifecycle (draft, active, archived); the sellable feed asks a separate
 * question and gets only what is live.
 *
 * COMPONENT PRICES ARE RESOLVED, NOT STORED. A bundle stores one target price for
 * the whole set; a component's REFERENCE unit price is whatever the selected
 * price list says today. The adapters therefore join the catalogue and the price
 * list themselves, and hand back a resolved figure (or `null` when the list
 * prices nothing for that unit) so the allocator upstream never re-reads storage.
 */
/**
 * A read that FAILED, as opposed to a read that found nothing.
 *
 * Both adapters raise this rather than returning an empty page, because the two
 * outcomes are opposite: an empty catalogue is a fact a storefront may cache and
 * show, and a failed query is not. The class lives on the port so neither chain
 * owns the distinction.
 */
export declare class BundleCatalogReadError extends Error {
    constructor(message: string);
}
/** Which lifecycle rows an admin list asks for. `all` is the admin default. */
export type BundleListStatusFilter = "draft" | "active" | "archived" | "all";
export interface ListBundlesInput {
    status: BundleListStatusFilter;
    /** Free-text match over code and title; absent means no narrowing. */
    query?: string;
    limit: number;
    offset: number;
}
export interface BundleSummary {
    code: string;
    title: string;
    status: string;
    fulfillmentMode: string;
    componentCount: number;
    hasActiveTargetPrice: boolean;
    updatedAt: string;
}
export interface ListBundlesResult {
    bundles: BundleSummary[];
    total: number;
}
/**
 * One line of the bill of materials, already joined to the catalogue.
 *
 * `productSlug` and `variantId` are carried because the availability port
 * addresses stock by all three coordinates; a caller that had only the sku would
 * have to re-open the catalogue to ask about stock, which is exactly the
 * round-trip this DTO exists to remove.
 */
export interface BundleComponentRow {
    sku: string;
    title: string | null;
    productSlug: string;
    variantId: string;
    quantity: number;
    isAddon: boolean;
    sortOrder: number;
    /** Resolved from the selected price list; `null` when it prices no such unit. */
    referenceUnitPriceMinor: number | null;
}
export interface BundleTargetPriceRow {
    mode: string;
    targetPriceMinor: number;
    currency: string;
    amountKind: string;
    active: boolean;
    validFrom: string;
    validTo: string | null;
}
export interface BundleDetail extends BundleSummary {
    /** The opaque `{kind, version, data}` envelope; `null` for an unconstrained bundle. */
    compositionConstraint: {
        kind: string;
        version: number;
        data: Record<string, unknown>;
    } | null;
    metadata: Record<string, unknown>;
    components: BundleComponentRow[];
    prices: BundleTargetPriceRow[];
    /** Present only when the host selects effective price authority. */
    resolvedTargetPrice?: BundleTargetPriceRow | null;
    /** The currency whose list resolved `referenceUnitPriceMinor`; `null` if none did. */
    resolvedCurrency: string | null;
    /**
     * The price list those reference figures came from. Carried because a price
     * preview has to name the list it priced against — an amount without the list
     * that gave it meaning is not reproducible.
     */
    resolvedPriceListId: string | null;
}
export interface GetBundleInput {
    code: string;
    /**
     * Price the components against THIS currency's active list. Omitted means "the
     * list this bundle's own active target price is written into", which is the
     * only currency an operator has actually chosen for it.
     */
    currency?: string;
    /** Narrow the target price whose list is selected; omitted means any mode (the opt-in effective store defaults to one_time). */
    mode?: string;
    regionCode?: string;
    atTime?: string;
}
/**
 * One live, sellable bundle: active status, an active target price, and its
 * components priced in that price's own currency. This is the feed's row, and it
 * is deliberately a different question from `getBundle` — the storefront must
 * never be able to see a draft by asking the read port nicely.
 */
export interface ActiveBundleComposition {
    code: string;
    title: string;
    fulfillmentMode: string;
    currency: string;
    targetPriceMinor: number;
    mode: string;
    components: BundleComponentRow[];
}
export interface BundleHistoryEvent {
    id: string;
    action: string;
    actorKind: string | null;
    actorEmail: string | null;
    entityId: string | null;
    oldValue: unknown;
    newValue: unknown;
    occurredAt: string;
}
export interface BundleHistoryInput {
    code: string;
    limit: number;
    offset: number;
}
export interface BundleHistoryResult {
    events: BundleHistoryEvent[];
    total: number;
}
export interface BundleCatalogReadPort {
    /** Admin list across the whole lifecycle, newest-agnostic and code-ordered. */
    listBundles(input: ListBundlesInput): Promise<ListBundlesResult>;
    /** Full admin detail, or `null` when no bundle carries that code. */
    getBundle(input: GetBundleInput): Promise<BundleDetail | null>;
    /**
     * Every bundle a storefront may sell right now. Never returns a draft or an
     * archived bundle, and never a bundle without an active target price.
     */
    listActiveBundleCompositions(input?: {
        currency?: string;
        regionCode?: string;
        mode?: "one_time" | "subscription";
        atTime?: string;
    }): Promise<ActiveBundleComposition[]>;
    /**
     * The operator trail for one bundle. The two chains answer from different
     * ledgers — a managed audit table and the platform's neutral write-event
     * relation — which is why this is a port method rather than a shared query.
     */
    listBundleHistory(input: BundleHistoryInput): Promise<BundleHistoryResult>;
}
```

## dist/catalog/bundleRuleCodes.d.ts

```ts
import type { BundleAllocationFailureCode } from "@openlup/core/pricing";
/**
 * Bundle write rules — the named, enforced invariants of the agent-operable
 * bundle write path. These codes are the single vocabulary shared by the request
 * contracts, the pure rule predicates (`bundleRules.ts`), managed write routines
 * and the over-the-wire error taxonomy. Managed routines re-derive state rules;
 * native actor-kind enforcement relies on the handler, not an independent
 * database gate.
 *
 * The catalog rules use this same shape; the lifecycle subset is expressed
 * through the host lifecycle kit, and the codes stay here as
 * this domain's registry.
 */
export declare const BUNDLE_RULE_CODES: readonly ["DRAFT_ONLY_FOR_MACHINE", "MIN_COMPONENTS", "ADDON_ONLY_COMPOSITION", "DUPLICATE_COMPONENT", "CODE_TAKEN", "COMPONENT_NOT_FOUND", "COMPONENT_NOT_SELLABLE", "COMPONENT_CURRENCY_MISMATCH", "TARGET_ABOVE_COMPONENT_SUM", "TARGET_BELOW_FLOOR", "PRICE_REQUIRED_TO_SELL", "COMPOSITION_REQUIRED_TO_SELL", "APPEND_ONLY_PRICE", "NO_HARD_DELETE", "RESTORE_REQUIRES_ARCHIVED", "DEACTIVATE_REQUIRES_ACTIVE", "FULFILLMENT_MODE_UNSUPPORTED", "CONSTRAINT_VALIDATION_FAILED", "CLONE_SOURCE_NOT_FOUND"];
export type BundleRuleCode = (typeof BUNDLE_RULE_CODES)[number];
/**
 * The two target-price codes are NOT re-derived here — they are the kernel
 * allocator's own failure vocabulary, projected onto this domain's registry.
 *
 * Declared as an exhaustive `Record` over `BundleAllocationFailureCode` on
 * purpose: when `@openlup/core/pricing` learns a new failure code, this object
 * stops compiling until the domain decides which rule it is. `REFERENCE_TOTAL_ZERO`
 * means the composition weighed nothing, which is the same condition
 * `MIN_COMPONENTS` names from the request side.
 */
export declare const BUNDLE_ALLOCATION_RULE_CODE: Record<BundleAllocationFailureCode, BundleRuleCode>;
/**
 * Which rules are decidable from the request alone (pure, pre-flight) and which
 * are state-dependent and authoritatively enforced by the write routine. This
 * documents intent and keeps the handler honest about what it can decide before
 * the write. `CONSTRAINT_VALIDATION_FAILED` is `port`: it is decided by the
 * injected `CompositionRulesPort`, which is neither the request nor the database.
 */
export declare const BUNDLE_RULE_ENFORCEMENT: Record<BundleRuleCode, "request" | "port" | "rpc">;
export type BundleActorKind = "human" | "machine";
export type BundleWriteOperation = "create_draft" | "update_draft" | "set_composition" | "set_target_price" | "archive" | "restore" | "clone_draft" | "activate" | "deactivate";
/** The domain's rule result, projected from the generic kit `RuleResult`. */
export type BundleRuleResult = {
    ok: boolean;
    violations: BundleRuleCode[];
};
```
## dist/catalog/bundleRules.d.ts

```ts
import type { BundleComponentInput } from "@openlup/core/pricing";
import type { CompositionConstraint, CompositionRulesPort } from "@openlup/core/bundle";
import type { BundleRuleBindings } from "./bundleBindings.js";
import { type BundleActorKind, type BundleRuleResult } from "./bundleRuleCodes.js";
/**
 * Pure, request-checkable bundle write rules.
 *
 * Only what is decidable from the request alone lives here. The state-dependent
 * rules (code uniqueness, component existence/sellability/currency, the target
 * price against real component prices, the activation preconditions, append-only
 * pricing, archive-not-delete) are re-derived by the managed write routines.
 * This layer fast-fails request-checkable violations without replacing those
 * state checks. Actor-kind enforcement is separate: managed publication routines
 * independently check it, while native publication relies on the handler gate.
 */
/** The composition shape the request-layer rules can reason about. */
export interface BundleCompositionEntry {
    readonly sku: string;
    readonly quantity: number;
    readonly isAddon: boolean;
}
/** The only fulfillment mode with a runtime. The column admits more; this does not. */
export declare const SUPPORTED_BUNDLE_FULFILLMENT_MODES: readonly ["virtual"];
export type SupportedBundleFulfillmentMode = (typeof SUPPORTED_BUNDLE_FULFILLMENT_MODES)[number];
/**
 * The lifecycle/actor gate every operation passes. Machine actors are confined to
 * drafts: they may compose, price, archive, restore and clone, but neither
 * activate nor deactivate.
 */
export declare function createBundleWriteRules({ assertActivateNotMachineActor }: Pick<BundleRuleBindings, "assertActivateNotMachineActor">): {
    evaluateBundleWriteRules: (input: {
        operation: string;
        actorKind: BundleActorKind;
    }) => BundleRuleResult;
};
/**
 * Whole-set composition rules, decidable from the replacement set alone: it must
 * carry at least one entry, at least one entry that is not an add-on, and no unit
 * twice (multiplicity is the quantity, never a repeated row).
 */
export declare function evaluateBundleComposition(components: readonly BundleCompositionEntry[]): BundleRuleResult;
/** The runtime refusal of every fulfillment mode the stored column admits but no code honours. */
export declare function evaluateBundleFulfillmentMode(mode: string): BundleRuleResult;
/**
 * Project the kernel allocator's decision onto this domain's rule vocabulary.
 *
 * The allocator (`@openlup/core/pricing`) owns the arithmetic and the two
 * refusals a target price can earn; this maps its failure code through the
 * exhaustive `BUNDLE_ALLOCATION_RULE_CODE` table so the codes cannot drift apart.
 * It is pure: the caller supplies the component reference prices it already holds.
 * The authority for a committed write remains the write routine, which holds the
 * live prices.
 */
export declare function evaluateBundleTargetPrice(input: {
    components: readonly BundleComponentInput[];
    targetPriceMinor: number;
    minimumUnitPayableMinor?: number;
}): BundleRuleResult;
/** An empty envelope means "unconstrained"; there is nothing for the port to decide. */
export declare function isEmptyCompositionConstraint(constraint: CompositionConstraint | null | undefined): boolean;
/**
 * Delegate a non-empty composition constraint to the injected rules port. The
 * platform stores the envelope and never interprets it, so this domain does not
 * either: it asks the adopter's port and reports the refusal under one code.
 */
export declare function evaluateBundleCompositionConstraint(port: CompositionRulesPort | undefined, input: {
    readonly components: readonly BundleCompositionEntry[];
    readonly constraint: CompositionConstraint | null | undefined;
}): Promise<BundleRuleResult>;
```

```ts
/** Plain bundle transport DTOs. The host owns parsing, defaults and wire-version values. */
export type SellableBundle = {
    code: string;
    title: string;
    fulfillmentMode: string;
    price: {
        amountMinor: number;
        currency: string;
    };
    components: {
        sku: string;
        quantity: number;
        effectiveUnitPriceMinor: number;
        lineSubtotalMinor: number;
        discountBps: number;
    }[];
    availability: {
        status: "unknown" | "available" | "low_stock" | "out_of_stock";
        sellableNow: number | null;
```

```ts
}
```

```ts
/**

/** Returns the product-scoped revision only when the declared current authority is sound. */
```

## dist/catalog/contracts/lifecycle.d.ts

```ts
/**
 * Lifecycle observations are additive DTOs, not changes to existing wire status.
 * SKU draft/active/archived and draft receipt open/abandoned remain unchanged.
 * Map archived to withdrawn only where an authority establishes that meaning;
 * abandoning a draft never implies withdrawing the active variant.
 */
export type CatalogVariantState = "draft" | "active" | "withdrawn";
export interface CatalogHeadRef {
    publicationId: string;
    revision: number;
    documentDigest: string;
}
export interface CatalogLifecycleObservation {
    productId: string;
    variantId: string;
    sku: string;
    state: CatalogVariantState;
    variantRevision: number;
    /** Null means known unpublished, never unknown publication lineage. */
    publicationHead: CatalogHeadRef | null;
    draftRef: {
        draftId: string;
        revision: number;
    } | null;
}
export interface CatalogPublishCommand {
    commandKey: string;
    productId: string;
    candidateRef: string;
    candidateDigest: string;
    expectedHead: CatalogHeadRef | null;
    changes: readonly {
        variantId: string;
        expectedRevision: number;
        state: "active" | "withdrawn";
    }[];
}
export interface CatalogPublicationReceipt {
    commandKey: string;
    fingerprint: string;
    actorId: string;
    previousHead: CatalogHeadRef | null;
    head: CatalogHeadRef;
    changes: readonly CatalogLifecycleObservation[];
}
/** Named refusals contain no arbitrary provider diagnostics. */
export interface CatalogPublicationRefusal {
    outcome: "unauthorized" | "validation_issue" | "conflict" | "dependency_unavailable" | "unsupported";
    reason: "publisher_not_authorized" | "candidate_invalid" | "head_conflict" | "publication_authority_unavailable" | "publication_not_supported";
}
/** Success requires coherent authoritative document and lifecycle facts. */
export type CatalogPublicationReadResult = {
    outcome: "found";
    head: CatalogHeadRef;
    documentRef: string;
    observations: readonly CatalogLifecycleObservation[];
} | {
    outcome: "unpublished";
} | CatalogPublicationRefusal;
/** Absence is distinct from an unavailable or unsupported authority. */
export type CatalogPublicationReceiptResult = {
    outcome: "found";
    receipt: CatalogPublicationReceipt;
} | {
    outcome: "absent";
} | CatalogPublicationRefusal;
/**
 * Authority-owned history, newest publication revision first with stable ordering.
 * Limit is an integer from 1 through 100; receipts never exceed limit.
 * nextCursor is opaque, and null only when no further page exists.
 */
export type CatalogHistoryResult = {
    outcome: "page";
    receipts: readonly CatalogPublicationReceipt[];
    limit: number;
    nextCursor: string | null;
} | CatalogPublicationRefusal;
```

## dist/catalog/draftValidation.d.ts

```ts
import type { CatalogDraftPayload } from "./draftSchemas.js";
import { type CatalogDraftIssue, type CatalogProductTypeRegistry } from "./productTypeSchemas.js";
export interface CatalogDraftReadiness {
    publication: {
        status: "incomplete" | "not_assessed";
        issues: CatalogDraftIssue[];
    };
    commercial: {
        status: "inactive";
        issues: CatalogDraftIssue[];
    };
}
export type CatalogDraftValidation = {
    valid: false;
    issues: CatalogDraftIssue[];
} | {
    valid: true;
    readiness: CatalogDraftReadiness;
};
/** Supplied facts must be true; absent publish-time facts remain draft readiness issues. */
export declare function validateCatalogDraft(payload: CatalogDraftPayload, registry: CatalogProductTypeRegistry): CatalogDraftValidation;
```

## dist/catalog/postgresBundleAdminWriteStore.d.ts

```ts
import { type PostgresDataGatewayEnv, type PostgresDataGatewayOptions } from "../data/index.js";
import type { AdminBundleWritePort } from "./adminBundleWritePort.js";
/**
 * Direct-Postgres adapter for the bundle write port: one role-free transaction per
 * operation, calling the same named routines under the same argument names as its
 * managed twin. It is a real adapter, not a placeholder — it opens a pooled
 * connection, runs the routine inside BEGIN/COMMIT, and surfaces the routine's own
 * SQLSTATE through the shared marshalling, so the port contract cannot tell the two
 * chains apart.
 *
 * The platform manifest creates no service role, so this lane never changes the
 * connection role; the adopter's own database user is the caller, exactly as the
 * outbox and delivery-receipt adapters do it.
 */
export interface PostgresBundleAdminWriteStore extends AdminBundleWritePort {
    close(): Promise<void>;
}
export declare function createPostgresBundleAdminWriteStore(env: PostgresDataGatewayEnv, options?: PostgresDataGatewayOptions): PostgresBundleAdminWriteStore;
```

```ts
import type { SellableCatalogItem, SellableCatalogProfile, CatalogSkuEnvelopeSelector, NeutralSkuEnvelopeV1 } from "./contracts/index.js";
/**
 * Minimal neutral catalog seam used by the local reference storefront. It is
 * independent of richer application catalog read ports.
 */
export interface SellableCatalogPort {
    getStorefrontProfile(): Promise<SellableCatalogProfile>;
    listSellableItems(): Promise<SellableCatalogItem[]>;
}
/**
 * Dark W2a read seam. Its row-safe return value deliberately contains document
 * references, never a document payload or a SKU-level document override.
 */
export interface CatalogSkuEnvelopeReadPort {
    listSkuEnvelopes(query: CatalogSkuEnvelopeListQuery): Promise<CatalogSkuEnvelopePage>;
    /**
     * Page only active trade items that are sellable in at least one commerce
     * channel. This is intentionally distinct from the neutral inventory-style
     * listing above: consumers such as compatibility must not discover dormant
     * SKUs and then try to filter them with a second authority.
     */
    listActiveSkuEnvelopes(query: CatalogActiveSkuEnvelopeListQuery): Promise<CatalogSkuEnvelopePage>;
    getSkuEnvelope(selector: CatalogSkuEnvelopeSelector): Promise<NeutralSkuEnvelopeV1 | null>;
    /** Compatibility lookup for callers that already persist the neutral SKU UUID. */
    getSkuEnvelopeById(skuId: string): Promise<NeutralSkuEnvelopeV1 | null>;
}
/**
 * Additive discovery capability. Existing commerce consumers remain typed to
 * {@link CatalogSkuEnvelopeReadPort} and therefore cannot accidentally use it.
 */
export interface PublicCatalogSkuEnvelopeReadPort extends CatalogSkuEnvelopeReadPort {
    /**
     * Public discovery's active-only page. Unlike the commerce method above it
     * intentionally includes temporarily unsellable active variants.
     */
    listPublicActiveSkuEnvelopes(query: CatalogSkuEnvelopeListQuery): Promise<CatalogSkuEnvelopePage>;
    /** Active-only cursor page restricted to one already-authoritative product. */
    listPublicActiveProductSkuEnvelopes(productId: string, query: CatalogSkuEnvelopeListQuery): Promise<CatalogSkuEnvelopePage>;
}
export interface CatalogSkuEnvelopeListQuery {
    cursor: string | null;
    limit: number;
}
export interface CatalogActiveSkuEnvelopeListQuery extends CatalogSkuEnvelopeListQuery {
    /** Optional server-selected scope; supplied selections contain 1–100 unique SKU codes. */
    skuCodes?: readonly string[];
}
export interface CatalogSkuEnvelopePage {
    items: NeutralSkuEnvelopeV1[];
    nextCursor: string | null;
}
```

## dist/catalog/sellableBundleHandler.d.ts

```ts
import type { HttpRequest, HttpResponse } from "../http/index.js";
import type { BffResponder } from "../bff/index.js";
import type { BundleResponseSchema } from "./adminBindings.js";
import type { SellableBundle } from "./contracts/bundleAvailability.js";
import type { BundleAvailability } from "./bundleAvailability.js";
import type { ActiveBundleComposition } from "./bundleCatalogReadPort.js";
import type { BundleAvailabilityService } from "./bundleAvailabilityService.js";
/**
 * The public sellable-bundle feed handler, shaped after the reference catalog
 * handler: GET-only, one
 * port call, validate the body before sending, and never serve an unvalidated
 * shape.
 *
 * It composes rather than computes twice: the availability service already folds
 * one batched stock answer into the live compositions, and the pricing kernel
 * turns the stored target price into per-component money. This module only decides
 * what a storefront is allowed to see.
 *
 * A bundle whose money cannot be derived is DROPPED from the feed, not published
 * with a guessed figure. Three cases reach that door — an unpriced component, an
 * empty composition, and a target price the kernel refuses — and all three mean
 * the same thing to a storefront: this is not sellable today. Publishing it with a
 * partial price would put a number on a shelf that no order line could reproduce.
 */
export interface SellableBundleHandlerDeps {
    availabilityService: BundleAvailabilityService;
    /** Narrow the feed to one currency; omitted means every currency an operator priced. */
    currency?: string;
    enabled: boolean;
}
export declare function createSellableBundleHandlers({ responder, sellableBundleListResponseSchema, SELLABLE_BUNDLE_CONTRACT_VERSION }: {
    responder: BffResponder;
    sellableBundleListResponseSchema: BundleResponseSchema;
    SELLABLE_BUNDLE_CONTRACT_VERSION: "catalog.sellable-bundle.v1";
}): {
    createSellableBundleHandler: (deps: SellableBundleHandlerDeps) => (req: HttpRequest, res: HttpResponse) => Promise<void>;
};
/**
 * Derive the row a storefront sees, or `null` when the money cannot be stated.
 *
 * The allocator emits TWO lines for a quantity whose subtotal does not divide
 * evenly, which is exactly why the feed carries lines rather than a unit price per
 * component: `unitPrice x quantity === lineSubtotal` holds on every line, and the
 * lines sum to the bundle's price with no remainder.
 */
export declare function toSellableBundle(composition: ActiveBundleComposition, availability: BundleAvailability): SellableBundle | null;
```

```ts
import { type PostgresDataGatewayEnv } from "./postgresGateway.js";

type Env = Record<string, string | undefined>;

} | null;

    asActor: <T>(claims: ActorClaims, work: (gateway: unknown) => Promise<T>) => Promise<T>;
};
export interface DataPortBindingOptions {
    /** Replaces the bindings' supabase-js `createClient` for this call (tests). */

}
export interface DataBindingDependencies {
    /** The supabase-js `createClient` used by every supabase gateway these bindings build. */
    createSupabaseClient: SupabaseCreateClient;
    /** Builds the direct-PostgreSQL gateway; defaults to `createPostgresDataGateway`. */
    createPostgresGateway?: (env: PostgresDataGatewayEnv) => DataGatewayPort;
}
export interface DataBindings {
    /** Resolve the DataGatewayPort for a bundle's declared data capability kind. */
    bindDataPort(dataKind: string, env: Env, requestOptions?: DataPortBindingOptions): DataGatewayPort | null;
    /** Bind one request to actor-only data access. */
    bindRequestActorDataPort(accessToken: string, env: Env, requestOptions?: DataPortBindingOptions): ActorOnlyDataPort | null;
    /** Resolve and bind the active bundle's data gateway, cached per bundle and data configuration. */
    bindBundleDataPort(env: Env): DataGatewayPort | null;
}
/** Create one set of data bindings; the bundle data port cache belongs to this set. */
export declare function createDataBindings(deps: DataBindingDependencies): DataBindings;
export {};
```

```ts
/** PostgREST-shaped error the consumers narrow on (`.code`, `.message`, `.details`, `.hint`). */
export interface PgShimError {
    code?: string;
    message?: string;
    details?: string;
    hint?: string;
}
export interface PgShimResult<T = unknown> {
    data: T;
    error: PgShimError | null;
    count?: number | null;
}
/** Minimal query surface we need from a pg client (Pool acquires a PoolClient per request). */
export interface PgQueryExecutor {
    query(text: string, values?: unknown[]): Promise<{
        rows: Record<string, unknown>[];
    }>;
}
export type Filter = {
    kind: "eq" | "gte" | "lte" | "gt";
    column: string;
    value: unknown;
} | {
    kind: "in";
    column: string;
    values: unknown[];
} | {
    kind: "ilike";
    column: string;
    value: string;
} | {
    kind: "is";
    column: string;
    value: unknown;
};
export interface OrderBy {
    column: string;
    ascending: boolean;
}
export declare function quoteIdent(raw: string): string;
export declare function renderColumns(columns: string): string;
export declare function renderFilter(filter: Filter, params: ParamAccumulator): string;
/**
 * PostgREST `count=exact` rendered as a SEPARATE aggregate over the same FROM/WHERE — deliberately
 * NOT a `COUNT(*) OVER()` folded into the row query. PostgREST's exact count ignores limit/offset,
 * and a window function emits no row at all for an empty page, so an offset past the end would
 * report 0 instead of the true total. Keeping it separate also leaves the row projection
 * byte-identical: there is no synthetic column to strip before cardinality shaping.
 */
export declare function renderCountQuery(table: string, filters: readonly Filter[], params: ParamAccumulator): string;
/**
 * PostgREST refuses an out-of-bounds page instead of answering an empty one -- but ONLY when it
 * knows the total, i.e. when an exact count was requested; without one it answers 200 and `[]`,
 * which is what this shim already did. The boundary is strict: offset === total is a legal empty
 * page, offset === total + 1 is not. Measured against PostgREST 14.10 before this was written,
 * including the `details` sentence, which carries the two numbers a caller pages on.
 */
export declare function rangeNotSatisfiable(offset: number | null, total: number | null): PgShimError | null;
/**
 * INSERT ... VALUES ... RETURNING, with the column list taken from the FIRST row and every later
 * row required to carry the same key set. Deriving the columns from row 0 alone and binding the
 * rest positionally is how a batch used to lose a column silently: an extra key vanished, a missing
 * one bound NULL over a DEFAULT. PostgREST answers PGRST102 "All object keys must match" to exactly
 * this payload -- and keeps answering it under `Prefer: missing=default`, measured -- so the shim
 * refuses too. Refusal, not union: parity is the point, and this builder exposes no Prefer header
 * through which a caller could ask for anything else.
 */
export declare function renderInsertStatement(table: string, rows: readonly Record<string, unknown>[], projection: string, params: ParamAccumulator): string;
export declare function unwrapRpcRows(rows: Record<string, unknown>[], functionName: string): unknown;
/** Accumulates values and hands back $1,$2,… placeholders — the single injection-safety chokepoint. */
export declare class ParamAccumulator {
    readonly values: unknown[];
    add(value: unknown): string;
}
export declare function joinParts(parts: string[]): string;
export declare function toShimError(error: unknown): PgShimError;
```

```ts
import { type Server } from "node:http";
import type { HttpRuntimePort } from "@openlup/core/platform-runtime";
import type { EntrypointLoader } from "./types.js";
import type { BffDispatch, RouteEntry } from "../bff/index.js";
import { type NodeSecurityHeaderPolicy, type StaticAssetReader } from "./security.js";
/** One deployment-specific standalone entrypoint, matched by its exact path. */
export interface HttpEntrypointRegistration {
    path: string;
    rawStream?: boolean;
    maxDurationMs?: number;
    load: EntrypointLoader;
}
/**
 * One deployment-specific entrypoint matched by a path rule. `match` returns the query values the
 * entrypoint receives for this path, or null when the path is not its own. The body is buffered.
 */
export interface HttpPathEntrypoint {
    match(pathname: string): Record<string, string> | null;
    load: EntrypointLoader;
    /** Request deadline; defaults to 10 seconds. */
    maxDurationMs?: number;
}
export interface NodeLegacyRedirect {
    source: string;
    destination: string;
    permanent: boolean;
}
export interface NodeCsrFallback {
    exactPaths: readonly string[];
    routeFamilies: readonly string[];
}
export interface NodeHttpRuntimeOptions {
    /** The deployment's BFF route table. */
    routes: readonly RouteEntry[];
    /** The dispatcher that matches `routes` (see `createBffDispatcher`). */
    dispatch: BffDispatch;
    /** Validated public-route contract supplied by the host composition root. */
    routing?: {
        legacyRedirects: readonly NodeLegacyRedirect[];
        ssgPaths: readonly string[];
        csrFallback: NodeCsrFallback;
    };
    /** Deployment header policy, including the content security policy. */
    security: NodeSecurityHeaderPolicy;
    /** robots.txt body for a request host; without it `/robots.txt` is served like any other path. */
    robotsTxt?: (host: string | undefined) => string;
    /**
     * Platform-path standalone handlers: the CSP report sink at `/api/csp-report` and the hosted
     * BFF router at `/api/bff-router`. A path without a handler is treated like any other path.
     */
    entrypoints?: {
        cspReport?: EntrypointLoader;
        bffRouter?: EntrypointLoader;
    };
    /**
     * Deployment exact-path handlers, checked after the CSP report entrypoint and before the BFF
     * router; a later registration replaces an earlier one with the same path.
     */
    extraEntrypoints?: readonly HttpEntrypointRegistration[];
    /** Deployment path-rule handlers checked before the BFF dispatcher, first match wins. */
    pathEntrypoints?: readonly HttpPathEntrypoint[];
    /** Deployment path-rule handlers checked after legacy redirects and before static documents. */
    fallbackPathEntrypoints?: readonly HttpPathEntrypoint[];
    /** Registry-derived allowlist; undeclared job ids must resolve null. */
    scheduledEntrypoint?: (jobId: string) => EntrypointLoader | null;
    /** Explicit host deadlines; missing cells use the bounded defaults below. */
    durations?: {
        bff?: number;
        cspReport?: number;
        bffRouter?: number;
        scheduled?: (jobId: string) => number;
    };
    /** Static SPA reader; without one, unmatched non-API paths answer 404. */
    staticReader?: StaticAssetReader;
    /** Structured error sink (defaults to console.error). */
    onError?: (context: string, error: unknown) => void;
}
/** Build the runtime's request handler (exported for unit tests; wraps every dispatch path). */
export declare function createRequestHandler(options: NodeHttpRuntimeOptions): HttpRuntimePort["handle"];
/** Create the Node HttpRuntimePort: `handle` for one request, `listen` to bind the server. */
export declare function createNodeHttpRuntime(options: NodeHttpRuntimeOptions): HttpRuntimePort & {
    server: Server | null;
};
```

```ts
import type { IncomingMessage, ServerResponse } from "node:http";
import type { HttpResponse } from "./types.js";
/** Deployment-owned header policy applied to every response of the Node runtime. */
export interface NodeSecurityHeaderPolicy {
    /** Headers set on every response unless the handler already set them, in this order. */
    readonly globalHeaders: ReadonlyArray<readonly [string, string]>;
    /** Static-asset path prefixes that receive the long-lived asset Cache-Control. */
    readonly assetCachePrefixes: readonly string[];
    /** Path families (the path or anything below it) that receive `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow`. */
    readonly noindexFamilies: readonly string[];
    /** Exact paths that receive `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow`. */
    readonly noindexExacts: readonly string[];
    /** True for a production host; every other host receives the strict non-production X-Robots-Tag. */
    readonly isProductionHost: (host: string | undefined) => boolean;
}
/** Apply the deployment's global and path-specific headers for this route. */
export declare function applySecurityHeaders(res: ServerResponse, pathname: string, host: string | undefined, policy: NodeSecurityHeaderPolicy): void;
/** Pluggable static reader so tests can inject an in-memory dist/ without touching the filesystem. */
export interface StaticAssetReader {
    /** Read a built asset (returns null if it does not exist). */
    read(relativePath: string): Promise<{
        body: Buffer;
        contentType: string;
    } | null>;
    /** The SPA fallback document (dist/index.html). */
    readIndex(): Promise<{
        body: Buffer;
        contentType: string;
    }>;
}
export declare function contentTypeFor(pathname: string): string;
/** A path "looks like" a built asset if it has a file extension (so we 404 missing assets, not SPA). */

/**
 * Try the initial asset read; return a found asset with 200. If the read misses
 * and the path looks like an asset, return 404. Otherwise, when
 * `allowRouteDocument` is true and its
 * lookup finds a route document, return that document with 200. If no route
 * document was returned, `allowFallback` true returns the SPA index with 200;
 * otherwise return 404. Both flags default to false. The host must enable these
 * options only for admitted routes; this helper does not identify application
 * routes.
 *
 * The middle step is not decoration. The build writes each pre-rendered route as
 * `<route>/index.html` and writes the HOME document to `index.html`; skipping that lookup hands
 * every admitted pre-rendered URL the home document instead of its own. The host
 * supplies the route-document and fallback decisions; this helper does not infer
 * application routes.
 */
export declare function serveSpaStatic(_req: IncomingMessage, res: HttpResponse, pathname: string, reader: StaticAssetReader, allowRouteDocument?: boolean, allowFallback?: boolean): Promise<void>;
```

```ts
import type { IncomingMessage, ServerResponse } from "node:http";
/** A Node request decorated with the parsed query, cookies and body. */
export interface HttpRequest extends IncomingMessage {
    query: Record<string, string | string[] | undefined>;
    cookies?: Record<string, string>;
    body?: unknown;
    /** Cooperative cancellation owned by the portable host adapter. */
    abortSignal?: AbortSignal;
}
/** A Node response decorated with the helper methods hosted functions provide. */
export interface HttpResponse extends ServerResponse {
    status(code: number): this;
    json(body: unknown): this;
    send(body: string | Buffer | object): this;
    redirect(url: string): this;
    redirect(status: number, url: string): this;
}
/** A default-export handler shared by every physical API entrypoint. */
export type HttpHandler = (req: HttpRequest, res: HttpResponse) => Promise<unknown> | unknown;
/** A lazily loaded module exposing one default-export handler. */
export type EntrypointLoader = () => Promise<{
    default: HttpHandler;
}>;
```

## dist/http/webhookSignature.d.ts

```ts
import type { HttpRequest } from "./types.js";
/** Maximum body size accepted by readRawBody, in bytes. */
export declare const MAX_WEBHOOK_BODY_BYTES = 1048576;
/** Thrown by readRawBody when its streamed or fallback body exceeds MAX_WEBHOOK_BODY_BYTES. */
export declare class WebhookBodyTooLargeError extends Error {
    constructor();
}
/**
 * Read an async-iterable request stream before consulting `req.body` and return
 * its chunks as concatenated UTF-8 text. Stream input is capped at
 * `MAX_WEBHOOK_BODY_BYTES`. Even a zero-byte chunk is a stream result. Only
 * when the stream emits no chunks, or no stream is available, fall back to a
 * string or Buffer in `req.body`, or JSON-serialize an object; fallback text is
 * capped by its UTF-8 byte length. Callers verifying signatures must preserve
 * original raw content, which may be exposed as a stream, string or Buffer; a
 * parsed object fallback cannot reconstruct the original wire bytes.
 */
export declare function readRawBody(req: HttpRequest): Promise<string>;
export declare function verifyHmacSignature(input: {
    rawBody: string;
    signatureHeader: string | undefined;
    secret: string;
}): boolean;
/**
 * Constant-time comparison of two shared-secret strings.
 *
 * Exported because it is not payment-specific: any route holding a shared token has to compare it
 * the same way, and a second hand-rolled copy of a security primitive is how one of them quietly
 * stops being constant-time. The length check comes first because `timingSafeEqual` THROWS on a
 * length mismatch rather than returning false, and the length of a shared token is not the secret.
 */
export declare function timingSafeStringEqual(left: string, right: string): boolean;
```

## dist/pricing/commercePricingPolicyErrors.d.ts

```ts
/** The server cannot assign a policy right now. Caller-independent: retrying an
 * identical request may succeed after server-side configuration or readiness changes. */
export declare class CommercePricingPolicyUnavailableError extends Error {
    readonly reason: string;
    constructor(reason: string);
}
/**
 * Every reason a caller-side refusal can carry. ⛔ Each one answers `400`, which
 * puts it outside every 5xx monitor, so each one owes a dedicated host monitor.
 * A host observability check can compare this tuple with its configured monitors;
 * the tuple itself does not install or verify those monitors. Adding a member
 * here without adding a monitor is the regression this tuple lets a host check
 * for: reclassifying `v2_capability_required` from `503` to `400` once silently
 * removed the only alert on a site-wide unpriceable-quote outage.
 */
export declare const PRICING_POLICY_REQUEST_REASONS: readonly ["v2_capability_required", "v2_visitor_id_required"];
export type PricingPolicyRequestReason = (typeof PRICING_POLICY_REQUEST_REASONS)[number];
/** The request itself lacks the context a v2 assignment needs. Caller-fixable,
 * and never evidence that pricing is down. The reason is typed off
 * PRICING_POLICY_REQUEST_REASONS so a host observability check can include it. */
export declare class CommercePricingPolicyRequestError extends Error {
    readonly reason: PricingPolicyRequestReason;
    constructor(reason: PricingPolicyRequestReason);
}
export type SafePricingPolicyReason = "v2_capability_required" | "v2_visitor_id_required" | "signing_secret_unavailable" | "v1_assignment_rejected" | "v2_assignment_config_invalid" | "v2_readiness_not_ready" | "other";
export interface PricingPolicyFailureMapping {
    code: "BAD_REQUEST" | "UPSTREAM_UNAVAILABLE";
    details: {
        reason: "pricing_policy_request_invalid" | "pricing_policy_unavailable";
        stage: "pricing_policy";
        policyReason: SafePricingPolicyReason;
    };
}
/**
 * Single owner of the status a pricing-policy refusal earns, shared by every
 * route that resolves one. A caller-attributable gap answers `400`; only a
 * server that genuinely cannot price answers `503`, so a `503` on these routes
 * stays a truthful outage signal.
 */
export declare function mapPricingPolicyFailure(error: unknown): PricingPolicyFailureMapping | null;
```
## dist/pricing/commerceQuoteBatchHandler.d.ts

```ts
import type { ResolvedAcquisitionTerms } from "./contracts/acquisitionTerms.js";
import type { HttpRequest, HttpResponse } from "../http/index.js";
import type { BffResponder } from "../bff/index.js";
import type { z } from "zod";
import { type CreateQuoteRequest, type CreateQuoteResponse } from "./contracts/quote.js";
import { COMMERCE_CONTRACT_VERSION } from "./contracts/index.js";
import { type CommerceQuotePort } from "./quotePorts.js";
import type { PricingPolicySnapshot } from "./contracts/offerPolicy.js";
export interface CommerceQuoteBatchHandlerDeps<Request extends CreateQuoteRequest = CreateQuoteRequest, Response extends CreateQuoteResponse = CreateQuoteResponse> {
    responder: BffResponder;
    acquisition?: {
        terms(quote: Response["quote"]): ResolvedAcquisitionTerms | undefined;
    };
    createQuoteBatchRequestSchema: z.ZodType<{
        quotes: Request[];
    }>;
    publicCreateQuoteBatchResponseSchema: z.ZodType<{
        contractVersion: typeof COMMERCE_CONTRACT_VERSION;
        quotes: Response["quote"][];
    }>;
    quotePort: CommerceQuotePort<Request, Response>;
    resolveCustomerEligibility?: (request: Request) => Promise<{
        clientId: string | null;
        source: "anonymous" | "resolved_client";
    }>;
    resolvePricingPolicy?: (request: Request) => PricingPolicySnapshot | undefined | Promise<PricingPolicySnapshot | undefined>;
}
/**
 * For each validated quote entry, resolves optional eligibility and policy and
 * then calls the injected quote port; entries run concurrently. It returns each
 * result's quote in request order, and a failed call fails the whole batch. This
 * handler supplies no shared timestamp or read context; any cross-entry sharing
 * belongs to the injected port. It returns quote bodies only and does not apply
 * the separate single-route acceptance or fallback steps.
 */
export declare function createCommerceQuoteBatchHandler<Request extends CreateQuoteRequest, Response extends CreateQuoteResponse>({ responder, createQuoteBatchRequestSchema, publicCreateQuoteBatchResponseSchema, quotePort, acquisition, resolveCustomerEligibility, resolvePricingPolicy, }: CommerceQuoteBatchHandlerDeps<Request, Response>): (req: HttpRequest, res: HttpResponse) => Promise<void>;
```

```ts
import type { CommerceCurrency, CommerceMoney, CommerceTaxBreakdown } from "./money.js";
import type { CommerceCodeRejectionDetail } from "./evidence.js";
export declare const COMMERCE_CONTRACT_VERSION = "commerce.v0";
export interface CommerceQuotePricingComponent {
    scope: "line" | "order";
    componentType: "base_unit" | "mode_discount" | "qty_tier" | "promo" | "loyalty" | "shipping" | "bundle";
    amountMinor: number;
    reasonCode: string;
    reasonPayload: Record<string, unknown>;
}
/** Hosts may extend this context with their own validated policy. */
export interface CommerceQuoteContext {
    mode: "one_time" | "subscription";
    cadenceDays?: number | null;
    promoCodes: string[];
}
export interface CommerceQuoteLine {
    sku: string;
    productSlug: string;
    quantity: number;
    unitPriceGross: CommerceMoney;
    lineSubtotalGross: CommerceMoney;
    tax: CommerceTaxBreakdown;
    pricingComponents?: CommerceQuotePricingComponent[];
}
export type CommercePromotionCustomerSemantic = "first_purchase_10" | "first_subscription_50" | "bundle_5";
export interface CommerceQuoteDiscount {
    promotionId: string;
    code?: string | null;
    label?: string;
    appliesTo: "order_total" | "line" | "shipping";
    amountOffMinor: number;
    reasonCode: string;
    /** Stable presentation identity for canonical offers; never inferred from an editable name. */
    customerSemantic?: CommercePromotionCustomerSemantic;
    promotionEngineVersion?: "promotion-engine.v2";
    promotionCodeId?: string;
    promotionCodeRevision?: number;
    promotionDefinitionFingerprint?: string;
    promotionCodeScopes?: Array<"one_time" | "subscription_initial">;
    promotionMinimumReferenceMinor?: number;
    promotionCodeValidTo?: string | null;
    promotionBenefitKind?: "target_percentage" | "percentage" | "fixed_amount" | "free_shipping";
    promotionBenefitValueBps?: number;
    promotionBenefitValueMinor?: number;
    floorApplied?: boolean;
}
/**
 * Why a customer-submitted promo code did not produce a discount, surfaced so the FE
 * can show a specific message instead of a generic "invalid". `better_price_exists` is
 * the one non-error reason (eligible but unused because an automatic offer is equally
 * good or better; the FE renders it neutrally); `scope_not_applicable` means the code
 * is real but does not apply to this order type. The last two are v2-engine-only reasons.
 */
export type CommerceCodeRejectionReason = "not_recognized" | "already_used" | "not_eligible" | "expired" | "better_price_exists" | "scope_not_applicable";
export interface CommerceCodeRejection {
    code: string;
    reason: CommerceCodeRejectionReason;
}
export interface CommerceQuote {
    currency: CommerceCurrency;
    taxIncluded: true;
    lines: CommerceQuoteLine[];
    discounts: CommerceQuoteDiscount[];
    pricingComponents?: CommerceQuotePricingComponent[];
    context?: CommerceQuoteContext;
    /** Submitted coupon codes that produced no discount, with the reason. Omitted when empty. */
    codeRejections?: CommerceCodeRejection[];
    /** UI-safe specifics for selected rejected codes. Omitted when no detail is available. */
    codeRejectionDetails?: CommerceCodeRejectionDetail[];
    subtotalGross: CommerceMoney;
    discountTotalGross: CommerceMoney;
    /** Gross shipping before any free-shipping promo. Optional; absent ⇒ shipping not priced (legacy/0). */
    shippingGross?: CommerceMoney;
    /** Gross shipping discount from a free-shipping promo (≤ shippingGross). Optional; absent ⇒ 0. */
    shippingDiscountGross?: CommerceMoney;
    totalGross: CommerceMoney;
    netTotal: CommerceMoney;
    taxTotal: CommerceMoney;
}
export interface CreateQuoteRequest {
    mode: "one_time" | "subscription";
    lines: import("./money.js").CartLineInput[];
    cadenceDays?: number | null;
    promoCodes: string[];
}
export interface CreateQuoteResponse {
    contractVersion: typeof COMMERCE_CONTRACT_VERSION;
    quote: Omit<CommerceQuote, "lines"> & {
        lines: Array<CommerceQuoteLine & {
            catalogFacts?: import("./provenance.js").CatalogFactsProvenance;
        }>;
    };
}
```

```ts
import type { CreateQuoteRequest } from "./contracts/quote.js";

import type { CommerceQuoteCatalogItem } from "./commerceQuoteCatalogReadPort.js";
/** Pure helpers for the DB-backed commerce quote port (extracted to keep the port under the LOC cap). */

    item: Item;
}
export declare function buildSkuIndex<Item extends CommerceQuoteCatalogItem>(quoteCatalogReadPort: {
    listQuoteCatalogItems(): Promise<Item[]>;
}): Promise<Map<string, SkuIndexEntry<Item>>>;
export declare function eligibleQtyForMode(request: CreateQuoteRequest, mode: Exclude<PricingMode, "any">): number;
export declare function resolveOneTimeComparison(input: {
    pricingResolverPort: PricingResolverPort;
    variantId: string;
    lineQty: number;
    eligibleCartQty: number;
    regionCode: string;
    currency: string;
    atTime: string;

export interface StrictQuotePriceResolution {
    base: ResolvedPrice;
    resolved: ResolvedPrice;
    policy?: {
        policyRevisionId: string;
        policyDigest: string;
    };
}
/**
 * Strict authority quotes require one exact one-time base anchor. Subscription money is either
 * derived by a validated policy or taken from the authority's explicit legacy
 * subscription observation; this layer never calls the generic resolver.
 */
export declare function resolveStrictQuotePrice(input: {
    commercePriceAuthorityPort: CommercePriceAuthorityPort;
    variantId: string;
    mode: Exclude<PricingMode, "any">;
    regionCode: string;
    currency: string;
    channel: string;
    atTime: string;
}): Promise<StrictQuotePriceResolution>;
export declare function assertSubscriptionCheapest(input: {
    requestSku: string;
    modeAtLine: Exclude<PricingMode, "any">;
    resolved: ResolvedPrice;
    oneTime: ResolvedPrice | null;
    vatRateBps: number;
}): void;
export declare function toGrossMinor(price: ResolvedPrice, vatRateBps: number): number;
export declare function calculateIncludedVat(grossMinor: number, vatRateBps: number): {
    netMinor: number;
    vatMinor: number;
};
/**
 * Map one kernel pricing component onto the quote transport shape.
 *
 * A component type outside the transport union stops the quote loudly. It is never
 * cast through and never silently dropped: a dropped component breaks the breakdown
 * invariant (components must sum to the line total), so a quote would go out priced
 * from an incomplete explanation of its own total.
 */
export declare function mapPricingComponent(component: PricingComponent, scope: "line" | "order"): CommerceQuotePricingComponent;
/**
 * A money builder bound to one currency.
 *
 * This used to be a plain `money(amountMinor)` that stamped the settlement
 * currency as a literal, which meant the quote's declared `currency` and the
 * currency on every amount inside it came from two different places. They
 * agreed only because the port's `currency` option was typed as that same
 * literal. Binding the builder once per port makes the agreement structural:
 * there is no second place left for the two to disagree.
 */
export declare function moneyIn(currency: string): (amountMinor: number) => CommerceMoney;
export declare function sum(values: number[]): number;
```

## dist/pricing/quotePorts.d.ts

```ts
import type { CreateQuoteRequest, CreateQuoteResponse } from "./contracts/quote.js";
import type { PricingPolicySnapshot } from "./contracts/offerPolicy.js";
/**
 * Server-resolved options for promo evaluation. Passed OUT-OF-BAND by the caller
 * (orchestration -> provisioned.clientId; preview -> session/null) — NEVER sourced
 * from the client request body — so `first_purchase` eligibility cannot be forged.
 */
export interface CreateQuoteOptions {
    clientId?: string | null;
    pricingPolicy?: PricingPolicySnapshot;
}
export interface CommerceQuotePort<Request = CreateQuoteRequest, Response = CreateQuoteResponse> {
    createQuote(request: Request, options?: CreateQuoteOptions): Promise<Response>;
    createServerAuthoritativeQuote?(request: Request, options?: CreateQuoteOptions): Promise<Response>;
}
export interface CommerceQuoteSnapshotVerifierPort<Response = CreateQuoteResponse> {
    verifyQuoteSnapshot(snapshot: Response): Promise<Response | void>;
}
export type CommerceQuoteSnapshotErrorCode = "QUOTE_SNAPSHOT_MISMATCH";
export declare class CommerceQuoteSnapshotError extends Error {
    readonly code: CommerceQuoteSnapshotErrorCode;
    readonly details: Record<string, unknown>;
    constructor(code: CommerceQuoteSnapshotErrorCode, message: string, details?: Record<string, unknown>);
}
export type CommerceQuoteErrorCode = "UNKNOWN_SKU" | "PRICE_NOT_CONFIGURED" | "UNSUPPORTED_TAX_PROFILE" | "PRICING_INVARIANT_VIOLATION";
export declare class CommerceQuoteError extends Error {
    readonly code: CommerceQuoteErrorCode;
    readonly details: Record<string, unknown>;
    constructor(code: CommerceQuoteErrorCode, message: string, details?: Record<string, unknown>);
}
```

```ts
import type { ShippingTerms } from "../shipping/index.js";

}
```
